# Session log 2026-11-13 — CT57 stacked branch

## 2026-11-13T01:45Z — CT57 rational-isogeny obstruction; cross-characteristic research lane

### What was done (commands, files, features)

- Read root PR #3877 and cross-characteristic PR #4015, AGENTS.md, CT55 universal division-field construction, CT56 Serre/Weil-pairing bound, the latest main-branch 2026-11-13 session log and cryptanalysis AGENTS.md. CT native Cantor changes were already in progress elsewhere; none touched here. crypto-autoresearcher AGENTS.md returned invalid UTF-8 in connector and was not relied upon.
- Shared KB tools and repository wrapper could not be invoked in this runtime; GitHub searches across crypto, cryptanalysis and crypto-autoresearcher and primary literature were fallback. Primary sources: Michaud-Jacobs' exposition of Mazur's rational-isogeny theorem (arxiv:2209.03153), Banwait et al. Theorem 1.1 (doi:10.4153/S0008414X22000244), Sage's small-prime isogeny documentation. No novel theorem is claimed.
- New `research/cross_characteristic_global_torsion_20261112/ct57/`: `CT57_RATIONAL_ISOGENY_GATE.md` and two independent arithmetic checks. Exact Python GL2 enumeration and independent JavaScript generator-orbit BFS both returned GL2 sizes 48/480/2016/13200, point stabilizers 6/20/42/110, line stabilizers 12/80/252/1100 and rejected confusing locally fixed Frobenius lines with globally invariant lines. Normal/optimized Python and normal/no-opt JS modes agreed byte-for-byte across implementations. Local only; [pcc:unverified].
- Added CT57 to bounded `.github/workflows/ct55-crosschar-audit.yml` including exact output comparison and explicit lack-of-Mazur-proof warning. Commits on existing #4015 branch: 88f35f7, 7fc5535, cd0b67b, 9c61e60, and notation correction 6c62100. No paid jobs. Local standalone reproducer:
  `python3 -B research/cross_characteristic_global_torsion_20261112/ct57/ct57_borel_audit.py`;
  `python3 -O -B research/cross_characteristic_global_torsion_20261112/ct57/ct57_borel_audit.py`;
  `node research/cross_characteristic_global_torsion_20261112/ct57/ct57_independent.js`;
  `node --no-opt research/cross_characteristic_global_torsion_20261112/ct57/ct57_independent.js`.

### Decisions made (and why)

- For any engineered E/Q and prime ell>163, a **globally Galois-stable elliptic ell-line** is impossible by Mazur: stable line iff rational ell-isogeny. This excludes only a specific low-image simplification, **not** a full division-field lift, a line rational over K != Q, or a higher-dimensional simple Jacobian factor.
- A source point rational modulo p only supplies an eigenvector of Frobenius_p; this does not imply a G_Q-stable line. CT57 tests the distinction independently.
- Remain on PR #4015 branch, not root #3877 or active cryptanalysis branches, to avoid conflicting with concurrent native Jacobian work.
- No claim of full Galois image, verified math signature, runtime speedup or scalable encoded-input cross-characteristic transfer. No expensive experiment or EKS admission.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

- Check exact-head CT57 Actions and scientific PCC status, and arrange independent theorem/prose review.
- Construct an encoded-input global torsion lift for arbitrary P,Q using changing K and genuine genus >=2 destination; compute orbit degree, coefficient heights, selected places and complete cold cost.
- Keep stacking and merge protections: PR #4015 is stacked on #4012. Main has a distinct concurrently edited 2026-11-13 log; reconcile before eventual merge, preserving both sets of entries.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

- Initial report wrote double-escaped math backslashes; corrected in 6c62100 before final report.
- Shared KB, conductor and authenticated lab checkout not present; GitHub connector and local, bounded stdlib arithmetic were used. Root branch does not yet contain the main branch's 2026-11-13 log, so this entry is scoped to the CT57 feature branch rather than overwriting another agent's main-branch log.

## 2026-11-13T02:57Z — CT58 cyclotomic full-division-field obstruction

### What was done (commands, files, features)

- Inspected current PR #3877 and its native Cantor/norm consumer status; PR #4015 and CT51–57; repository AGENTS.md; dated main and feature-branch 2026-11-13 logs; cryptanalysis AGENTS.md. crypto-autoresearcher AGENTS.md again returned a UTF-8 decoding error, so it was not used. No native/Jacobian files changed.
- Consulted primary external mathematical references: Anthony Snowden, `https://websites.umich.edu/asnowden/teaching/2013/679/L02.html` (Weil pairing and cyclotomic determinant), Martin Orr, `https://www.martinorr.name/blog/2011/09/06/weil-pairings-the-skew-symmetric-pairing/` (polarized abelian varieties), and earlier CT55–57 reports. These are classical theorems; no novelty claim.
- Shared KB MCP tools absent; repository wrapper and conductor not executable without a checkout. Three GitHub searches were the partial fallback, across the crypto, cryptanalysis and crypto-autoresearcher repositories; absence not asserted.
- Added `ct58/CT58_CYCLOTOMIC_FULL_TORSION_GATE.md`, separate Python/JavaScript bounded arithmetic checks, and a CT58 step to the existing five-minute `.github/workflows/ct55-crosschar-audit.yml`. Locally ran Python normal/optimized and JS normal/no-opt, compared identical numeric output (excluding final annotation). 15 prime residue-degree controls and Frobenius 11a1 mod2 fixed-point counts 5/5/5/25 passed; finite GL2 symplectic checks for ell=3,5,7 passed. The Python finite identity was strengthened after noticing its first check was tautological; rerun passed. All claims remain [pcc:unverified].
- No paid compute and no full Rust/Lean validation in this restricted connector runtime. Reproduction commands are in CT58 note.

### Decisions made (and why)

- By nondegenerate Weil pairing, if full `A[ell]` is rational over L for a principally polarized abelian variety over any number field, then `Q(zeta_ell) subset L` and `[L:Q] >= ell-1`, **even if the global family depends on the ECDLP input**. Good reduction over 2 also forces `ord_ell(2) | f_w`. This constrains CT55's full division-field materialization; it does NOT prohibit a single oriented point or encoded-input ECDLP lift.
- The 11a1 rational 5-torsion example explicitly distinguishes one point (already rational at binary residue degree 1) from the complete basis (first rational at degree 4), preventing an incorrect extension of the lower bound to single-point lifting.
- Keep work on the existing cross-characteristic PR #4015 branch, stacked on #4012, and leave root/native PR #3877 and other active repository work untouched.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

- Inspect exact-head CT58 CI and scientific PCC status. Main and this feature branch's Nov 13 session logs differ; reconcile on merge without overwriting concurrent entries.

## 03:57 UTC — sparse differential filter characterized exactly

### What was done (commands, files, features)

New NormWronskian module compiles10 theorems. For monic separable G and
natDegree N<=natDegree G, W_G(N)=G*N'-G'*N vanishes exactly when N is a scalar
multiple of G. Nonzero N forces nonzero scalar. The actual norm-lift corollary
has the same feasible matrices. Additional proofs give the norm derivative,
the genuine norm degree cap, monic/separable split-node products, and an F17
ghost N=X*(X-1)^17 that passes W_X but is not cX [pcc:unverified].

### Decisions made (and why)

This is an exact test of a concrete proposed constraint filter, not empirical
scaling. The sparse operator remains a possible implementation representation,
but cannot be described as a stronger constraint or as eliminating rank one.
No characteristic assumption beyond the field is needed for the main theorem;
separability and the actual degree cap suffice.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

Actual-source gate/fresh replay, native expression identity record, proof map,
visual report and PR. Matrix-search algorithmic structure remains the next
research target; do not end the renewed search as blocked after this test.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

Build001 failed in the finite-field fixtures because the field instance needed
a prime Fact and the contradiction needed an explicit nonzero numeral. Builds
002/003 exposed an unreduced natural-number cast under polynomial C. Used the
existing ZMod.natCast_self lemma; build004 passed3145 jobs with standard axioms.
Failed logs are retained and not counted as successful proofs.

## 04:27 UTC — cofactor recovery and exact rejection controls compile

### What was done (commands, files, features)

NormDualPencil proves rank<=1 implies all entry minors vanish, corank-one
singular S has adjugate rank<=1, and a nonzero symmetric adjugate annihilating
all quadratic-form pairings recovers a nonzero quadratic witness. The exact
2x2 norm pencil has checked determinant/cofactor formulas. Positive F17 target
q=10,y=6 recovers slope15 from an adjugate column and satisfies the actual norm.
Negative target q=0,y=6 gives the same singular S but fails its cofactor tests;
its recovered slope also fails the actual norm. A diagonal 3x3 control has
zero adjugate/all cofactor tests zero and no nonzero primal witness. Build005
passed3147 jobs with standard axioms [pcc:unverified].

This is a sufficient recovery certificate, not proof that such a certificate
always exists in the coupled-Hankel family or can be found quickly. Both dual
completeness/regularity and constructive search cost remain explicit. Do not
count determinant roots or higher-corank critical points as recovered witnesses.

## 2026-11-13T04:25Z — fes-f2-gpu: FES GPU worker in a real relation phase (Claude)

### What was done (commands, files, features)

- User: hook the FES GPU worker into a real relation phase. Built `gpu/fes --serve` (CUDA and host
  workers, protocol in `fes_io.hpp`), `ServeWorker` and a shared server in `src/cryptanalysis/fes_gpu.rs`,
  and the solver engine `fes-f2-gpu` (`ic_framework/solvers.rs`; `FesF2` body factored into
  `fes_quadratic_solve`, so counted cost is identical by construction).
- Tests (`cargo test --release --lib`): serve protocol vs host worker (40 systems), Gray-order sort
  (60 systems), `fes_gpu_engine_counts_like_fes_f2`, every-engine agreement on random and descent
  systems: all pass. Local smoke `ic bench` at Koblitz n=13 with the host serve worker: verified.
- A100 pod `02rw1tw9o5v1hm` (03:31Z–04:20Z, ~$1.80, deleted): `ic bench --sweep` Koblitz n=23,
  char-2 n=25 and n=29 (`research/ic_gpu_e2e_20261113`). After the order fix, `fes-f2-gpu` equals
  `fes-f2` in calls, hit rate and S; whole solve 2.18 s vs 26.8 s (n=23), 3.22 s vs 141.9 s (n=25);
  38.7 s vs `inherited-f4` 895.5 s (n=29). Counted S unchanged; `inherited-f4` stays cheapest.
- Record `benchmarks/records/20261113T0330Z-ic-fes-gpu-e2e.json` closed; REPORT.md/.pdf,
  `fig_e2e_wall.svg`, `RESULTS.md`, raw data.
- Sources checked: KB unavailable (no research-kb server in this session);
  `research/inherited_f4_next_20261112/RESULTS_AB.md` and `RESULTS_E2E.md`, `AGENTS.md` §6.

### Decisions made (and why)

- Wall time reported as a practicality note only (AGENTS.md §6); no scoreboard or leaderboard row,
  because S does not move.
- GPU proposals sorted by Gray rank: the descent oracle keeps the first liftable solution, and the
  first run (arbitrary kernel order) took a different trajectory (2642 vs 1878 calls).
- No size rule proposed: in counted units `inherited-f4` wins at every size measured.
- `rp_gpu.sh` host CUDA-version filter made optional: with it, every GPU type reported no capacity.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

- Per-call overhead (1.0–3.6 ms vs 0.1–1.3 ms kernel): binary protocol, batching several systems per launch.
- Independent replay of the e2e rows.

## 2026-11-13T18:01Z — correction: the worker's "request parse" timer included the idle wait; the parse is not the next cut [pcc:unverified] (Claude)

### What was done (commands, files, features)

- Chased the 160–320 us "read" phase of the `gpu/fes` serve worker with a host worker on the real pipe: the figure did not change with `scanf` versus a hand parser (159 versus 160 us), with 1 or 4 Rayon threads, or under `taskset` to two CPUs. `strace -f` on the worker showed one `read(0)` of about 1 KB per request, so the request was not arriving in pieces. [pcc:unverified]
- Cause: the worker's idle-skipping peek was one `getc`/`ungetc`. The parser leaves the request's trailing newline in the stream, so the next peek returns at once, the timer starts, and the whitespace skip then blocks until the next request. The phase therefore contained the wait for the Rust side. Replay of the captured 400 requests through the same host worker: pre-buffered stream 6.6 us per call; a stream with a 600 us gap between requests 714 us. With the peek made to skip whitespace, the real-pipe host figure is 13.3, 13.0 and 15.1 us with the `scanf` parse and 4.4, 4.6 and 4.2 us with the hand parser (19 unknowns, 786 calls, alternating order). [pcc:unverified]
- Fixed the same peek in `gpu/fes/fes.cu` (`FES_SERVE_TIMING`). Corrected `REPORT.typ`/`REPORT.pdf`, `fig_a100.svg` and the A100 record `20261113T1642Z-ic-gpu-call-overhead-a100` (metric names `worker_wait_and_read_*`, notes and summary); progress report regenerated.

### Decisions made (and why)

- Withdrew the claim that the request parse is the largest worker phase and the next cut. A hand parser would save about 9 us of a 389 us call (about 2 percent); it was not committed. The hand parser, its equivalence test (300 random streams, mutation-checked) and a microbenchmark are kept only in the scratchpad.
- The A100 worker phase figures other than the first column (qsym, copies, kernel, reply) do not depend on the peek and stand. About 0.2 ms of the 0.374 ms A100 round trip is not located by any phase timer; that is the next candidate, but its split (pipe wake-up latency, stdio, parse) is unmeasured on the GPU and needs another Modal run, which needs a checkpoint token or a new waiver (the single-run waiver is used).

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

- Timestamp both sides of the pipe on the GPU host to split the 0.2 ms. PR #4062 body updated to carry the correction; auto-merge was enabled earlier at 17:53 UTC, check-in at 18:44 UTC.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

- My own timer was wrong, and the previous entry and the PR body built a conclusion on it. Found by replaying one request stream with and without gaps; corrected in the report, record, figure, session log and PR body, with the original entry above left as written.

## 16:50Z — Limits of the JV route; Koblitz n29 row re-read; J2 (quartic DL1 factor-base size) local pilot (Claude)

### What was done
- PR #3978 (class-group walk): the `pcc-gate` prose lint failed on four PR-body lines ("weak curve",
  "improvement") without `[pcc:unverified]`; body fixed (`tools/pcc/pcc.py lint-prose --stdin`: 0 findings).
  The body edit did not start a new gate run, so this merge of main and this entry are the push that re-runs it.
- Answered "are we at the theoretical limit" for the JV weak-class route (desk estimates, `[pcc:unverified]`):
  a walk needs about p²/3 curves (class ≈ p³, weak density ≈ 3/p²), which fits 68% at p = 37 and 0/… at
  p ≥ 1009 under cap 3,000; the route's ceiling is the genus-3 IC cost ≈ p^{8/3} against rho ≈ p³, and it
  never reaches prime-order curves (#E mod 4 is an isogeny-class invariant).
- Re-read the Koblitz f2m29 orbit-IC row (1.27× rho-frob, `research/ecbench_lazy_orbit_members_20261018`):
  28 of 32 workloads sit at 1.5–2.2×; the mean is pulled down by four legacy-rho cap-loss outliers
  (interval [0.77, 1.80]). The generator-orbit lever is already measured and validated
  (`research/ecbench_generator_orbit_20261022`, n29 below rho-frob, gap grows with r), and
  `research/leads_20261112` (lane K) closes the two-summand Koblitz lane at exponent 2/3. Not pursued.
- J2 (`LEAD-J-dl1-percolation-fb-size`, `research/leads_20261112`): built the unchanged N4 v2 driver
  (`rustc --edition 2021 -O research/quartic_n4v2_20261020/v2.rs`) and ran a local pilot (not grid data,
  L0, 4-core container, single attempt with the driver's built-in +0.25 retry): D family, c_D ∈
  {1.40, 1.50, 1.60, 1.75, 2.00}, q = 65479, 262111, 1048507, seeds 1–3.

### Decisions made
- Stop the Koblitz-n29 line (lever done, lane closed by the exponent-2/3 bound); run J2 locally as a pilot,
  because it is the cheapest lead predicted to give a measured improvement on the only below-rho IC family.
- Pilot only; any leaderboard claim needs a frozen protocol and the isolab-eks route (AGENTS.md §9a).

### Open threads
- Pilot result (unverified): c = 1.50 fails 9/9 units, c = 1.60 succeeds 9/9; DL1 M-eq at c = 1.60 / c = 2.00 =
  1.03 (k16), 0.75 (k18), 0.68 (k20); DL 0.88, 0.72, 0.67. At k16 the descent phase eats the gain (known
  points halve, descent trials 19 → 251, ≈ 3.5M M-eq, about constant in q). Second pilot running:
  c ∈ {1.55, 1.60, 1.70, 1.80, 2.00} × k = 18, 20, 22 × seeds 1–5.
- Next: a size-aware rule (c near threshold for k ≥ 18) or incremental factor-base growth instead of the

## 2026-11-13 ~21:30 UTC — Fix: the filter-layout default broke an ecbench replay sentinel (Claude)

### What was done (commands, files, features)
- After PR isogeny-labs/crypto#4073 auto-merged, its `replay_sentinels_pr`
  failed on
  `research/ecbench_registry_optimization_20261101/execution/smoke-koblitz-descent`,
  seq 14: "differs in total_gae, phase counts, phase gae, counters". It
  had passed on the PR before (4068), so the failure is this change's.
- Root cause: a filter layout lets different absent keys through, so the
  counted bucket reads change. Answers do not change, but ecbench prices
  those counters, and records counted under Low no longer replay under
  Top.
- Reproduced with `ecbench verify --replay-seq 14` at main `5612511494`:
  reproduced=false by default, true with `KIC_FILTER_LAYOUT=low`.
- Fix:
  - added `PairSumTable::build_within_for_layout`;
  - pinned `ecbench`'s `koblitz_descent` and `koblitz_batched` to
    `FilterLayout::Low`, the layout every committed record was counted
    under;
  - `ic price` and the other runtime paths keep `Top`.
- Verified: seq 14 reproduces (also with `KIC_FILTER_LAYOUT=top` forced),
  and the full sentinel script gives 51 sessions, 90 records, 0 failing.
  Clippy is clean.
- Removed the merged, clean `/home/user/wt-v9` worktree (its head is in
  main) and the artifact cache to free disk; the first sentinel attempt
  hit ENOSPC.

### Decisions made (and why)
- Pin the replayed harness rather than revert the default. The build gain

## 2026-11-13 — prime-field continuation after the whole-call audit (Codex)

### What was done (commands, files, features)

### Decisions made (and why)

- Demoted the approximately 140 GB `D(8,3)` completion from first priority to a degree diagnostic.
  The later cost-floor result shows that `D=9` alone cannot support the advertised single-target gain.
- Ranked actual sparse positive relation finding first. If one invocation costs `B^(gamma+o(1))`, the
  optimistic all-output model can beat rho only for `gamma < 7/2`; a one-output solver needs
  `gamma < 3`. Solving degree is not accepted as a substitute for this complete-work measurement.
- Ranked the signed norm-branch determinant as the next Boolean/tensor falsifier. Its Laplace expansion
  has every coefficient on the applicable middle layer nonzero under the distinct-x/nonzero-y guards,
  so exact balanced coefficient ranks are the cheapest test for genuine compression.
- Kept ordinary SAT/SMT and trimmed proof-size measurements conditional on a mechanism gate. Existing
  hashed and Semaev Boolean contraction evidence is negative, and short UNSAT proofs alone do not price
  positive relation finding.
- Kept research-kb standing unavailable; no novelty claim was made.

### What is complete

- Read-only evidence reconciliation and the prospective experiment/decision rules are recorded on branch
  `codex/prime-field-continuation-20261113`.
- A development-only exact arithmetic sanity check at p=17,d=6 reproduced all 15 nonzero level-2
  sign coefficients, levels 1 and 2 after fixing the target sign, and the piecewise PKM exponent formula
  on a dense beta grid. It is not a scientific scaling run or retained evidence artifact.
- No scientific run, benchmark record, pcc claim, leaderboard update, or security conclusion was produced.

  build, test, exact rank census, solver panel, EKS submission, or replay was possible.
- The missing L1 native closure source remains a prerequisite for a replayable PKM panel unless an

## 2026-11-13 — norm-branch ordinate-rank invariance correction (Codex)

### What was done (commands, files, features)

- Re-read the signed determinant's Laplace coefficients by split/cardinality block after merging PR #4077.

  scalings to each block, so the randomized-ordinate arm in the new protocol cannot be a statistical null.
- Updated `CONTINUATION_PROTOCOL.md` to classify that arm as an exact invariance control, compare only
  against an independently randomized-coefficient null, and place a compound-Vandermonde full-rank
  theorem/counterexample gate before the SAT panel.

  x_i=i+1, d=5..18. Every fixed-cardinality block reached the rectangular upper rank bound. No source,
  output artifact, scaling claim, or retained scientific row is attached to this check.

  a held-out rank gap. If the compound-Vandermonde blocks are always full rank under the guarded
  hypotheses, exact linear-message coefficient compression is closed without solver tuning.
- Randomized ordinates remain useful as a deterministic implementation control: any rank difference is
  an instrument defect.
